### PR #152133: gh-151593: Avoid deadlock in insert_split_key().

- URL: [https://github.com/python/cpython/pull/152133](https://github.com/python/cpython/pull/152133)
- State: closed; closed (not merged): 2026-06-25T01:59:57Z
- Author: nascheme
- Changes: +114 / -1 across 3 file(s)

#### Description

```text
We must not acquire other locks while holding the LOCK_KEYS() mutex.  This issue is fixed by calling PyType_Modifed() after releasing the mutex.

Note: this PR was assisted by Claude Opus 4.8.

<!-- gh-issue-number: gh-151593 -->
* Issue: gh-151593
<!-- /gh-issue-number -->
```

#### Changed Files

##### `Lib/test/test_free_threading/test_dict.py` (modified; +103 / -0)

```diff
@@ -336,5 +336,108 @@ def reader():
         with threading_helper.start_threads([t1, t2]):
             pass
 
+
+@threading_helper.requires_working_threading()
+class TestDictDeadlock(TestCase):
+    """Regression test for the free-threaded deadlock fixed in gh-151593.
+
+    The deadlock needs several threads and was only triggered intermittently by
+    normal test runs (e.g. ``test_abc --parallel-threads=10``); this test sets up
+    the required roles directly so a regression shows up reliably.
+
+    The bug: ``insert_split_key()`` called ``PyType_Modified()`` while still holding
+    the dictobject.c shared-keys lock (``LOCK_KEYS``).  ``PyType_Modified()`` wants
+    the typeobject.c ``TYPE_LOCK``, so a thread could hold ``LOCK_KEYS`` and block on
+    ``TYPE_LOCK`` -- a lock-ordering inversion.  The fix moves the
+    ``PyType_Modified()`` call to after ``LOCK_KEYS`` is released.
+
+    The cycle that hangs:
+
+      * one mutator thread holds ``LOCK_KEYS`` and waits for ``TYPE_LOCK``
+        (``insert_split_key`` -> ``PyType_Modified``);
+      * the "stopper" thread holds ``TYPE_LOCK`` and runs ``types_stop_world()``
+        (here via assigning ``__abstractmethods__``), waiting for every thread to
+        reach a safe point;
+      * the remaining mutator threads are parked acquiring ``LOCK_KEYS`` without
+        detaching, so they never reach a safe point and the world never stops.
+
+    The stopper therefore never finishes and never drops ``TYPE_LOCK``, the first
+    mutator never makes progress, and ``LOCK_KEYS`` is never released.  Several
+    mutator threads are required so that some are blocked on ``LOCK_KEYS`` while
+    another holds it.
+    """
+
+    # Each round operates on one fresh class so that ``insert_split_key()`` keeps
+    # taking the slow path that inserts a brand new shared key.
+    NROUNDS = 100
+
+    # Mutator threads racing to insert new shared keys / allocate inline values.
+    NMUTATORS = 4
+
+    def test_insert_split_key_vs_type_modified(self):
+        box = [None]
+        # +1 for the type-modifying "stopper" thread, +1 for the main thread
+        # which produces a fresh class for each round.
+        nparties = self.NMUTATORS + 2
+        round_start = Barrier(nparties)
+        round_end = Barrier(nparties)
+
+        def fresh_class():
+            class C:
+                pass
+
+            # Prime the type: give it a non-zero tp_version_tag (so that the
+            # later PyType_Modified() actually takes the type lock instead of
+            # returning early) and create its shared keys / inline values.
+            c = C()
+            c.warm = 0
+            getattr(c, "warm")
+            return C
+
+        def mutator(tid):
+            for _ in range(self.NROUNDS):
+                round_start.wait()
+                cls = box[0]
+                # Allocating exercises _PyObject_InitInlineValues (LOCK_KEYS);
+                # setting fresh attributes exercises insert_split_key, whose
+                # first insertion invalidates the type while holding LOCK_KEYS.
+                obj = cls()
+                setattr(obj, f"x{tid}_a", 1)
+                setattr(obj, f"x{tid}_b", 2)
+                setattr(obj, f"x{tid}_c", 3)
+                round_end.wait()
+
+        def stopper():
+            for _ in range(self.NROUNDS):
+                round_start.wait()
+                cls = box[0]
+                # type_set_abstractmethods() holds the type lock and calls
+                # types_stop_world().  An empty set keeps the class
+                # instantiable while still stopping the world.
+                cls.__abstractmethods__ = frozenset()
+                cls.__abstractmethods__ = frozenset()
+                cls.__abstractmethods__ = frozenset()
+                round_end.wait()
+
+        with threading_helper.catch_threading_exception() as cm:
+            threads = [
+                Thread(target=mutator, args=(i,))
+                for i in range(self.NMUTATORS)
+            ]
+            threads.append(Thread(target=stopper))
+            for t in threads:
+                t.start()
+
+            for r in range(self.NROUNDS):
+                box[0] = fresh_class()
+                round_start.wait()
+                round_end.wait()
+
+            for t in threads:
+                t.join()
+
+            self.assertIsNone(cm.exc_value)
+
+
 if __name__ == "__main__":
     unittest.main()
```

##### `Misc/NEWS.d/next/Core_and_Builtins/2026-06-24-15-52-19.gh-issue-151593.bWdDi1.rst` (added; +3 / -0)

```diff
@@ -0,0 +1,3 @@
+Fix deadlock in ``insert_split_key()``.  We must not acquire other locks
+while holding the ``LOCK_KEYS()`` mutex.  This issue is fixed by calling
+:c:func:`PyType_Modified` after releasing the mutex.
```

##### `Objects/dictobject.c` (modified; +8 / -1)

```diff
@@ -214,6 +214,7 @@ set_values(PyDictObject *mp, PyDictValues *values)
     _Py_atomic_store_ptr_release(&mp->ma_values, values);
 }
 
+// Note: you must not acquire other locks while holding this.
 #define LOCK_KEYS(keys) PyMutex_LockFlags(&keys->dk_mutex, _Py_LOCK_DONT_DETACH)
 #define UNLOCK_KEYS(keys) PyMutex_Unlock(&keys->dk_mutex)
 
@@ -1923,21 +1924,27 @@ insert_split_key(PyDictKeysObject *keys, PyObject *key, Py_hash_t hash)
     }
 #endif
 
+    bool inserted = false;
     LOCK_KEYS(keys);
     ix = unicodekeys_lookup_unicode(keys, key, hash);
     if (ix == DKIX_EMPTY && keys->dk_usable > 0) {
         // Insert into new slot
         FT_ATOMIC_STORE_UINT32_RELAXED(keys->dk_version, 0);
-        _PyDict_SplitKeysInvalidated(keys);
         Py_ssize_t hashpos = find_empty_slot(keys, hash);
         ix = keys->dk_nentries;
         dictkeys_set_index(keys, hashpos, ix);
         PyDictUnicodeEntry *ep = &DK_UNICODE_ENTRIES(keys)[ix];
         STORE_SHARED_KEY(ep->me_key, Py_NewRef(key));
         split_keys_entry_added(keys);
+        inserted = true;
     }
     assert (ix < SHARED_KEYS_MAX_SIZE);
     UNLOCK_KEYS(keys);
+    if (inserted) {
+        // This may result in TYPE_LOCK being acquired and we are not allowed
+        // to acquire other locks inside LOCK_KEYS.
+        _PyDict_SplitKeysInvalidated(keys);
+    }
     return ix;
 }
 
```

#### PR Discussion

**nascheme**, 2026-06-25T01:59:31Z:

```text
This fixes the deadlock but it introduces a correctness issue: a thread can read a stale class attribute/method back from an instance attribute it just stored.  The only solution I can think of for this now is to revert GH-150489. :-(
```

### PR #152200: gh-151593: Fix dead lock in PyDict insert_split_key()

- URL: [https://github.com/python/cpython/pull/152200](https://github.com/python/cpython/pull/152200)
- State: closed; merged: 2026-06-25T17:58:27Z; merge commit: [`bef570622263`](https://github.com/python/cpython/commit/bef570622263ecd58563f0474693c19c8545de73)
- Author: vstinner
- Changes: +18 / -1 across 1 file(s)

#### Description

````text
Do not hold LOCK_KEYS() lock when calling PyType_Modified() to avoid a deadlock.

<!--
Thanks for your contribution!
Please read this comment in its entirety. It's quite important.

# Pull Request title

It should be in the following format:

```
gh-NNNNNN: Summary of the changes made
```

Where: gh-NNNNNN refers to the GitHub issue number.

Most PRs will require an issue number. Trivial changes, like fixing a typo, do not need an issue.

# Backport Pull Request title

If this is a backport PR (PR made against branches other than `main`),
please ensure that the PR title is in the following format:

```
[X.Y] <title from the original PR> (GH-NNNNNN)
```

Where: [X.Y] is the branch name, for example: [3.13].

GH-NNNNNN refers to the PR number from `main`.

-->


<!-- gh-issue-number: gh-151593 -->
* Issue: gh-151593
<!-- /gh-issue-number -->
````

#### Changed Files

##### `Objects/dictobject.c` (modified; +18 / -1)

```diff
@@ -214,6 +214,15 @@ set_values(PyDictObject *mp, PyDictValues *values)
     _Py_atomic_store_ptr_release(&mp->ma_values, values);
 }
 
+// gh-151593: The _Py_LOCK_DONT_DETACH flag ensures that the outer critical
+// section is not dropped if there is some contention on the keys lock.
+// It also means that it will be important that LOCK_KEYS() is essentially the
+// "inner-most" code and that we don't call Py_DECREF() or similar while
+// holding the keys lock.
+//
+// We are not allowed to acquire other locks within LOCK_KEYS(). For example,
+// PyType_Modified() must not be called within LOCK_KEYS() since it acquires
+// the type lock.
 #define LOCK_KEYS(keys) PyMutex_LockFlags(&keys->dk_mutex, _Py_LOCK_DONT_DETACH)
 #define UNLOCK_KEYS(keys) PyMutex_Unlock(&keys->dk_mutex)
 
@@ -1923,12 +1932,13 @@ insert_split_key(PyDictKeysObject *keys, PyObject *key, Py_hash_t hash)
     }
 #endif
 
+    bool inserted = false;
     LOCK_KEYS(keys);
     ix = unicodekeys_lookup_unicode(keys, key, hash);
     if (ix == DKIX_EMPTY && keys->dk_usable > 0) {
         // Insert into new slot
+        inserted = true;
         FT_ATOMIC_STORE_UINT32_RELAXED(keys->dk_version, 0);
-        _PyDict_SplitKeysInvalidated(keys);
         Py_ssize_t hashpos = find_empty_slot(keys, hash);
         ix = keys->dk_nentries;
         dictkeys_set_index(keys, hashpos, ix);
@@ -1938,6 +1948,13 @@ insert_split_key(PyDictKeysObject *keys, PyObject *key, Py_hash_t hash)
     }
     assert (ix < SHARED_KEYS_MAX_SIZE);
     UNLOCK_KEYS(keys);
+
+    if (inserted) {
+        // gh-151593: Calling PyType_Modified() with LOCK_KEYS() creates a
+        // deadlock. So only call the function after UNLOCK_KEYS().
+        _PyDict_SplitKeysInvalidated(keys);
+    }
+
     return ix;
 }
 
```

#### Review Comments

##### `Objects/dictobject.c`

**dpdani**, 2026-06-25T13:47:01Z:

```text
Can you also add a comment here explaining why the flag is important and the ramifications it has?

Some points:
- `_Py_LOCK_DONT_DETACH` prevents the outer CS to get released while the keys' mutex is locked
- must not acquire more locks in this CS (must be the top-most critical section)
- @colesbury maybe you want to expand on the rationale for this as well?
```

#### PR Discussion

**vstinner**, 2026-06-25T13:33:14Z:

```text
On Python built with `./configure --with-pydebug --with-system-expat --with-thread-sanitizer --disable-gil`, I can easily reproduce the issue with:

`./python -m test test_abc --parallel-threads=10 -v -F`

It only takes 1 or 2 iterations to trigger the deadlock.

With this change, I can no longer reproduce the deadlock: the test is running for 5 minutes (test_abc passed 35 times in a row).
```

**vstinner**, 2026-06-25T13:37:01Z:

```text
@dpdani @colesbury @nascheme: Would you mind to review this fix?

I just converted @nascheme's [comment](https://github.com/python/cpython/issues/151593#issuecomment-4732702741) into an actual PR.
```

**vstinner**, 2026-06-25T14:31:29Z:

```text
@dpdani: I added a comment on LOCK_KEYS() based on previous comments on pull requests and issues. Does it look good to you?
```

### PR #152232: gh-152238: Revert gh-150490 and gh-152200.

- URL: [https://github.com/python/cpython/pull/152232](https://github.com/python/cpython/pull/152232)
- State: closed; merged: 2026-06-26T18:20:41Z; merge commit: [`9626ef87f42f`](https://github.com/python/cpython/commit/9626ef87f42fca564c3a74109b370d38076a79da)
- Author: nascheme
- Changes: +1411 / -1307 across 12 file(s)

#### Description

```text
Revert commits:
   gh-151593: Fix dead lock in PyDict insert_split_key() (#152200)
   gh-150490: Raise PyType_Modified for insertion into split dictionary (#150489)

For gh-150489, it violates locking discipline and results in deadlocks, gh-151593 is an example of it being hit in CI.  The attempted fix of gh-152200 avoids the deadlock but introduces a data-race.  The race window is small but can be triggered with pure Python code.

<!-- gh-issue-number: gh-152238 -->
* Issue: gh-152238
<!-- /gh-issue-number -->
```

```diff
 }
 
+static inline void
+_Py_atomic_store_ssize_release(Py_ssize_t *obj, Py_ssize_t value)
+{
+#if defined(_M_X64) || defined(_M_IX86)
+    *(Py_ssize_t volatile *)obj = value;
+#elif defined(_M_ARM64)
+    __stlr64((unsigned __int64 volatile *)obj, (unsigned __int64)value);
+#else
+#  error "no implementation of _Py_atomic_store_ssize_release"
+#endif
+}
+

@@ -990,6 +1002,18 @@ _Py_atomic_load_uint32_acquire(const uint32_t *obj)
 #endif
 }
 
+static inline Py_ssize_t
+_Py_atomic_load_ssize_acquire(const Py_ssize_t *obj)
+{
+#if defined(_M_X64) || defined(_M_IX86)
+    return *(Py_ssize_t volatile *)obj;
+#elif defined(_M_ARM64)
+    return (Py_ssize_t)__ldar64((unsigned __int64 volatile *)obj);
+#else
+#  error "no implementation of _Py_atomic_load_ssize_acquire"
+#endif
+}
+
 // --- _Py_atomic_fence ------------------------------------------------------
 
  static inline void
```

```diff
 static inline size_t
 shared_keys_usable_size(PyDictKeysObject *keys)
 {
+#ifdef Py_GIL_DISABLED
+    // dk_usable will decrease for each instance that is created and each
+    // value that is added.  dk_nentries will increase for each value that
+    // is added.  We want to always return the right value or larger.
+    // We therefore increase dk_nentries first and we decrease dk_usable
+    // second, and conversely here we read dk_usable first and dk_entries
+    // second (to avoid the case where we read entries before the increment
+    // and read usable after the decrement)
+    return (size_t)(_Py_atomic_load_ssize_acquire(&keys->dk_usable) +
+                    _Py_atomic_load_ssize_acquire(&keys->dk_nentries));
+#else
     return (size_t)keys->dk_nentries + (size_t)keys->dk_usable;
+#endif
```


````text
I think we want to use `_Py_LOCK_DONT_DETACH`. Otherwise, locking the keys object can release an outer critical section. This also means that it will be important that `LOCK_KEYS()` is essentially the "inner-most" code and that we don't call `Py_DECREF()` or similar while holding the keys lock. 


```suggestion
#define LOCK_KEYS(keys) PyMutex_LockFlags(&keys->dk_mutex, _Py_LOCK_DONT_DETACH)
```
````

**colesbury**, 2024-01-31T20:10:26Z:

```diff
@@ -140,6 +140,31 @@ To avoid slowing down lookups on a near-full table, we resize the table when
 it's USABLE_FRACTION (currently two-thirds) full.
 */
 
+#ifdef Py_GIL_DISABLED
+
+#define LOCK_KEYS(keys) PyMutex_Lock(&keys->dk_mutex)
+#define UNLOCK_KEYS(keys) PyMutex_Unlock(&keys->dk_mutex)
+
+#define ASSERT_KEYS_LOCKED(keys) assert(PyMutex_IsLocked(&keys->dk_mutex))
+#define LOAD_SHARED_KEY(key) _Py_atomic_load_ptr_acquire(&key)
+#define STORE_SHARED_KEY(key, value) _Py_atomic_store_ptr_release(&key, value)
+// Inc refs the keys object, giving the previous value
+#define INCREF_KEYS(dk)  _Py_atomic_add_ssize(&dk->dk_refcnt, 1)
```

```text
I think we want to avoid locking the shared keys when using them to create dicts.

We're OK with a possibly stale "usable size" as long as it's no smaller than the true size. The `shared_keys_usable_size` monotonically decreases (good), but is split across two fields, which makes it a bit more complex.
```

```diff
+#define UNLOCK_KEYS(keys) PyMutex_Unlock(&keys->dk_mutex)
+
+#define ASSERT_KEYS_LOCKED(keys) assert(PyMutex_IsLocked(&keys->dk_mutex))
+#define LOAD_SHARED_KEY(key) _Py_atomic_load_ptr_acquire(&key)
+#define STORE_SHARED_KEY(key, value) _Py_atomic_store_ptr_release(&key, value)
+// Inc refs the keys object, giving the previous value
```

````text
We can use a weaker ordering here that will be faster, especially on x86 where "release" doesn't require any memory barrier:

```
    _Py_atomic_store_ssize_relaxed(&keys->dk_nentries, keys->dk_nentries + 1);
    _Py_atomic_store_ssize_release(&keys->dk_usable, keys->dk_usable - 1);
```

(I don't think we have `_Py_atomic_store_ssize_release` yet, though)

I find the memory orderings hard to reason correctly about, so I like to model them with [CDSChecker](https://plrg.ics.uci.edu/software_page/42-2/). Here's the model I used for this:
https://github.com/colesbury/c11-model-checker/blob/cpython-models/test/gh-112075.c
````

```diff
+
+static inline void
+init_keys_object(PyDictKeysObject* dk, uint8_t log2_size, int log2_bytes, int kind,
+                 Py_ssize_t usable, Py_ssize_t entry_size)
+{
 #ifdef Py_REF_DEBUG
     _Py_IncRefTotal(_PyThreadState_GET());
 #endif
     dk->dk_refcnt = 1;
     dk->dk_log2_size = log2_size;
     dk->dk_log2_index_bytes = log2_bytes;

@@ -837,23 +827,59 @@ new_keys_object(uint8_t log2_size, bool unicode)

     memset(&dk->dk_indices[0], 0xff, ((size_t)1 << log2_bytes));
     memset(&dk->dk_indices[(size_t)1 << log2_bytes], 0, entry_size * usable);
+}
+
+static PyDictKeysObject*
+new_keys_object(uint8_t log2_size, bool unicode)
+{
+    Py_ssize_t usable = USABLE_FRACTION((size_t)1<<log2_size);
+    size_t entry_size = unicode ? sizeof(PyDictUnicodeEntry) : sizeof(PyDictKeyEntry);
+
+    int log2_bytes = get_log2_bytes(log2_size);

+    if (log2_size == PyDict_LOG_MINSIZE && unicode) {
+        dk = _Py_FREELIST_POP_MEM(dictkeys);

+                          + entry_size * usable);

+            return NULL;
+        }

+    init_keys_object(dk, log2_size, log2_bytes,
+                     unicode ? DICT_KEYS_UNICODE : DICT_KEYS_GENERAL,
+                     usable, entry_size);

         _Py_FREELIST_FREE(dictkeys, keys, PyMem_Free);

-        PyMem_Free(keys);
+        PyMem_Free(ptr);
     }
 }
```

````text
I'm able to reproduce the issue on my Fedora 44 laptop.

* Python built with `./configure --cache-file=../python-config.cache --with-pydebug --with-system-expat --with-thread-sanitizer --disable-gil`

For example, test_ABC_helper() of test_abc hangs.

* Thread 40 waits for `_PyEval_StopTheWorld()`.
* Thread 38 waits for `_PyThreadState_Attach()`.
* Thread 1 waits for `threads.join()` (wait until tests complete)
* Thread 2 is TSan background thread.
````

### Comment 5 — vstinner, 2026-06-17T13:02:14Z

Source: [https://github.com/python/cpython/issues/151593#issuecomment-4730459135](https://github.com/python/cpython/issues/151593#issuecomment-4730459135)

````text
Thread 40 calls `type_set_abstractmethods()`, extract:

```c
    BEGIN_TYPE_LOCK();
    type_modified_unlocked(type);
    types_stop_world();  // <========== HERE
    if (abstract)
        type_add_flags(type, Py_TPFLAGS_IS_ABSTRACT);
    else
        type_clear_flags(type, Py_TPFLAGS_IS_ABSTRACT);
    types_start_world();
    ASSERT_TYPE_LOCK_HELD();
    END_TYPE_LOCK();
```

First, it acquires the type lock. Then, it calls `_PyEval_StopTheWorld()`.

`BEGIN_TYPE_LOCK()` was added by commit baae9cb159e240ee9474ce7c02f88c233390777a.

```
commit baae9cb159e240ee9474ce7c02f88c233390777a
Author: Neil Schemenauer <nas-github@arctrix.com>
Date:   Tue Feb 25 21:24:20 2025 -0800

    gh-117657: Use an atomic store to set type flags. (gh-127588)
    
    The `PyType_HasFeature()` function reads the flags with a relaxed atomic
    load and without holding the type lock.  To avoid data races, use atomic
    stores if `PyType_Ready()` has already been called.
```

`types_stop_world()`  was added by commit fbbbc10055e0ee0011cf683132be706815480839:

```
commit fbbbc10055e0ee0011cf683132be706815480839
Author: Neil Schemenauer <nas-github@arctrix.com>
Date:   Tue May 27 18:27:41 2025 -0700

    gh-127266: avoid data races when updating type slots (gh-133177)
    
    In the free-threaded build, avoid data races caused by updating type
    slots or type flags after the type was initially created.  For those
    (typically rare) cases, use the stop-the-world mechanism.  Remove the
    use of atomics when reading or writing type flags.
```
````

### Comment 7 — vstinner, 2026-06-17T13:13:06Z

Source: [https://github.com/python/cpython/issues/151593#issuecomment-4730568699](https://github.com/python/cpython/issues/151593#issuecomment-4730568699)

```text
@nascheme @colesbury @kumaraditya303: It seems like this issue is a dead lock issue.

`type_set_abstractmethods()` holds the type lock and waits for `types_start_world()`, whereas other threads are waiting for the type lock and are not suspended by the stop-the-world request. At the end, all threads are waiting for an event, but nothing happens since they are blocking each others.
```

````text
I reproduced with `./configure --with-pydebug --disable-gil` and `./python -m test test_abc --parallel-threads=10` on main (a173a6d65ba0eb77776ca4f7f1cb277877a43f9b).

I can't understand why `_Py_LOCK_DONT_DETACH` would be necessary for `LOCK_KEYS`, and there's no comment to explain it either. Furthermore, the test suite runs fine with this change, which would also fix the issue at hand:

diff --git a/Objects/dictobject.c b/Objects/dictobject.c
index ac2f210d023..c476bc30e3e 100644
--- a/Objects/dictobject.c
````

````text
Author: Dino Viehland <dinoviehland@meta.com>
Date:   Thu Jun 11 09:38:31 2026 -0700

    gh-150490: Raise PyType_Modified for insertion into split dictionary (#150489)

```

That's why I cannot reproduce it on the 3.15 branch.
````

### Comment 12 — colesbury, 2026-06-17T14:48:16Z

Source: [https://github.com/python/cpython/issues/151593#issuecomment-4731763932](https://github.com/python/cpython/issues/151593#issuecomment-4731763932)

```text
1) The `_Py_LOCK_DONT_DETACH` is important. It ensures that the outer critical section is not dropped if there is some contention on the keys lock
2) We are not allowed to acquire other locks within `LOCK_KEYS()`. The referenced commit violates that locking discipline, which leads to deadlock.
```

### Comment 16 — nascheme, 2026-06-17T16:14:06Z

Source: [https://github.com/python/cpython/issues/151593#issuecomment-4732702741](https://github.com/python/cpython/issues/151593#issuecomment-4732702741)

````text
The "referenced commit" Sam's talking about is this one: https://github.com/python/cpython/commit/efb2fffae1fc728a5467672e6781cdf2df9bfec3.  The lock being acquired inside `LOCK_KEYS()` is done by the call to `PyType_Modified()`.  Possible solution is to move the invalidation out of the LOCK_KEYS region. E.g. something like this:

```
static Py_ssize_t
insert_split_key(PyDictKeysObject *keys, PyObject *key, Py_hash_t hash)
{
    assert(PyUnicode_CheckExact(key));
    Py_ssize_t ix;
#ifdef Py_GIL_DISABLED
    ix = unicodekeys_lookup_unicode_threadsafe(keys, key, hash);
    if (ix >= 0) {
        return ix;
    }
#endif
    bool inserted = false;
    LOCK_KEYS(keys);
    ix = unicodekeys_lookup_unicode(keys, key, hash);
    if (ix == DKIX_EMPTY && keys->dk_usable > 0) {
        FT_ATOMIC_STORE_UINT32_RELAXED(keys->dk_version, 0);
        Py_ssize_t hashpos = find_empty_slot(keys, hash);
        ix = keys->dk_nentries;
        dictkeys_set_index(keys, hashpos, ix);
        PyDictUnicodeEntry *ep = &DK_UNICODE_ENTRIES(keys)[ix];
        STORE_SHARED_KEY(ep->me_key, Py_NewRef(key));
        split_keys_entry_added(keys);
        inserted = true;
    }
    assert (ix < SHARED_KEYS_MAX_SIZE);
    UNLOCK_KEYS(keys);
    if (inserted) {
        _PyDict_SplitKeysInvalidated(keys);  // <-- PyType_Modified() called inside here
    }
    return ix;
}
```
````

### [cc36e3c471a4_dict_split_key_race_demo.py.txt](https://github.com/user-attachments/files/29352335/dict_split_key_race_demo.py.txt)

```text
"""Reproducer for the gh-151593 split-key insert / type-version race.

Run on a free-threaded (Py_GIL_DISABLED) build.  NO C changes required: this
hits the real bug in the committed gh-151593 branch code.

    ./python dict_split_key_race_demo.py [rounds] [nstoppers]

What the bug is
---------------
gh-151593 moved ``_PyDict_SplitKeysInvalidated()`` (-> ``PyType_Modified()``,
which bumps ``tp_version_tag``) to *after* ``UNLOCK_KEYS`` in
``insert_split_key()`` to avoid a lock-order deadlock.  That opens a window: a
thread can first-insert a new shared key, publish it, and another thread can
observe the key and store/read an instance value for it *before* the type
version is bumped.  A ``LOAD_ATTR_NONDESCRIPTOR_WITH_VALUES`` site (which only
guards on the type version + inline-values valid bit, since gh-150490 deleted
its ``_GUARD_KEYS_VERSION``) then returns the cached *class* attribute instead
of the instance value that was just stored.

Why it is built this way
------------------------
  * Free-threaded CPython gives each thread its OWN specialized bytecode copy
    (TLBC).  So each WRITER thread must warm ITS OWN reader copy to
    ``LOAD_ATTR_NONDESCRIPTOR_WITH_VALUES`` while the key is absent.  Warming in
    another thread does nothing for this thread.
  * The class attribute (``SENTINEL``) is a fresh ``type``: a non-descriptor with
    a deferred refcount, which the free-threaded specializer requires.
  * A fresh code object (via ``exec``) per round gives each round a clean site.
  * The bare ``UNLOCK_KEYS -> PyType_Modified`` window is only a few ns.  The
    "stopper" threads hammer a *separate* class's ``__abstractmethods__`` to hold
    the per-interpreter ``TYPE_LOCK`` across a ``types_stop_world()``, delaying
    the winner's bump so a loser can land inside the window.  (That is the same
    contention that produced the original deadlock.)  With no stoppers the window
    is effectively unhittable -- so the count of stale reads tracks the widening.

Oracle: a writer stores ``obj.foo = <distinct value>`` then immediately reads it
back through its own specialized site (store sequenced-before read, same thread).
Reading the class SENTINEL back is an unambiguous correctness violation.
"""

import sys
from threading import Barrier, Thread

NROUNDS = int(sys.argv[1]) if len(sys.argv) > 1 else 2000
NSTOPPERS = int(sys.argv[2]) if len(sys.argv) > 2 else 4
NWRITERS = 4
WARM = 8
KEY = "foo"

if not hasattr(sys, "_is_gil_enabled") or sys._is_gil_enabled():
    sys.exit("needs a free-threaded build with the GIL disabled")


def make_reader():
    ns = {}
    exec(
        "def read(o):\n return o.%s\n" % KEY, ns
    )  # fresh code object per round
    return ns["read"]


stop_all = [False]


def stopper():
    class Dummy:
        pass

    Dummy()
    while not stop_all[0]:
        try:
            Dummy.__abstractmethods__ = frozenset()
        except Exception:
            pass


box = {}
bugs = []  # (round, tid, stored, read_back)


def writer(tid):
    for _ in range(NROUNDS):
        box["start"].wait()
        sentinel = box["sentinel"]
        reader = box["reader"]
        obj = box["objs"][tid]
        val = box["vals"][tid]
        # 1) warm THIS thread's own copy while KEY is absent -> NDV cached at V
        for _ in range(WARM):
            reader(obj)
        box["race"].wait()
        # 2) race: store, then read back through our own NDV site
        setattr(obj, KEY, val)
        got = reader(obj)
        if got is sentinel:
            bugs.append((box["round"], tid, val, got))
        box["end"].wait()


def main():
    box["start"] = Barrier(NWRITERS + 1)
    box["race"] = Barrier(NWRITERS)
    box["end"] = Barrier(NWRITERS + 1)

    stoppers = [Thread(target=stopper, daemon=True) for _ in range(NSTOPPERS)]
    writers = [Thread(target=writer, args=(i,)) for i in range(NWRITERS)]
    for t in stoppers + writers:
        t.start()

    for r in range(NROUNDS):
        sentinel = type(
            "SENTINEL_%d" % r, (), {}
        )  # non-descriptor, deferred refcount
        C = type("C", (), {KEY: sentinel})
        box["round"] = r
        box["sentinel"] = sentinel
        box["reader"] = make_reader()
        box["objs"] = [C() for _ in range(NWRITERS)]
        box["vals"] = [["value-%d-%d" % (r, i)] for i in range(NWRITERS)]
        box["start"].wait()
        box["end"].wait()

    stop_all[0] = True
    for t in writers:
        t.join()

    print(
        "rounds=%d writers=%d stoppers=%d -> stale reads = %d"
        % (NROUNDS, NWRITERS, NSTOPPERS, len(bugs))
    )
    if bugs:
        r, tid, stored, got = bugs[0]
        print(
            "  e.g. round %d writer %d stored %r but read back the class "
            "attribute %r (stale type version)"
            % (r, tid, stored, got.__name__)
        )
        print("RACE REPRODUCED (this is a bug)")
        return 1
    print(
        "no stale reads observed "
        + (
            "(try more rounds / stoppers)"
            if NSTOPPERS
            else "(window not widened)"
        )
    )
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())
```
